- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_node_casting.py`.
- v0.8 notes: the agreed decision asked to "remind the user that the change can be undone in the Data Editor"; the implementation says "Nothing was changed." instead (the failed cast changes nothing, so there is nothing to undo). Confirm with Chao which wording v0.8 should use. DuckDB `TRY_CAST`/`strptime` errors differ from Polars; keep the sentences, map DuckDB's errors to them.

#### V07-114 Dates: plain date format panel, year-first display, simple entry (#205 item 6)

- Commits: a49163ab
- Area: date/datetime conversion panel (Data Editor and Builder), all table cells and Row Details, date entry fields (Filter etc.)
- Type: wording + display behaviour change
- Problem / reason: the panel said "Provide a strftime format or let Auto Fill guess it", "Use Python strftime codes.", "Could not infer format", "Inferred."; tables showed "2020-01-30T00:00:00.000Z"; the placeholder was "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS+00:00".
- What changed:
  - `DatetimeFormatPanel`: description "How are the dates written?"; body "How are the dates written? Wordflow can work it out from the data, or you can enter the format." plus "In this column: {up to 3 distinct sample values}". Field label "Date format" (old "Custom format"), placeholder "for example %d/%m/%Y" (old "e.g., %Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"), monospace. Button "Detect from the data" (old "Auto Fill"). Status (aria-live): failure "Couldn't work out the date format. Enter it above, or choose an example below."; success "Worked out from the data.". Examples list with text "Examples (choose one to use it). %d is the day, %m the month number, %b the short month name, %Y the year, %H the hour and %M the minute."; clicking an example fills the format: "30/01/2020" %d/%m/%Y; "2020-01-30" %Y-%m-%d; "30 Jan 2020" %d %b %Y; "January 30, 2020" %B %d, %Y; "30/01/2020 14:05" %d/%m/%Y %H:%M; "2020-01-30 14:05:00" %Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S (aria "Use {format} for dates like {example}"). Default target label "date and time".
  - Display: `displayDateTime(text)` turns the decoder's exact ISO output (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.sssZ`) into "YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM", adding seconds only when not :00 and milliseconds only when not .000; no time zone shown (Wordflow stores UTC). Other text unchanged. Applied in Data Editor cells (`formatCellValue`), Row Details, Concordance table, Builder preview table, Annotation results. Data values keep the ISO text that charts and filters parse.
  - Entry: placeholder "YYYY-MM-DD or YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM"; `normalizeIsoDraft` accepts "2020-01-30 14:05" (space converted to T).
  - Help ui.md updated.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A timestamp 2020-01-30T00:00:00.000Z shows as "2020-01-30 00:00"; 2020-01-30T14:05:07.000Z shows "2020-01-30 14:05:07".
  - Typing "2020-01-30 14:05" in a date filter is accepted.
  - With samples that cannot be inferred, the panel shows the "Couldn't work out the date format…" line; clicking "30/01/2020" fills %d/%m/%Y.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/lib/displayDateTime.ts` (new), `common/components/DatetimeFormatPanel.tsx` (`DATE_FORMAT_EXAMPLES`, `handleDetect`), `preprocessing/utils/dateTimeHelpers.ts` (`ISO_PLACEHOLDER`, `normalizeIsoDraft`), `formatCellValue.ts`, `RowDetailPanel.tsx`, `concordanceTableModel.ts`, `PreviewTable.tsx`, `AnnotationResultsPanel.tsx`.
- Tests: `lib/__tests__/displayDateTime.test.ts`, `preprocessing/utils/__tests__/dateTimeHelpers.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: formats are Python/Polars strftime codes; DuckDB `strptime` uses almost the same `%` codes, but check `%b`/`%B` and fractional seconds. The display rewrite depends on the Arrow JS decoder producing `toISOString()` text; v0.8 should format dates at display time only, never in stored data.

#### V07-137 Data Editor tools stay open after Apply; Close button (#217)

- Commits: ddc50f2a
- Area: Data Editor
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Every Data Editor tool closed on Apply, so chaining edits (for example Clean text "Remove digits" and then "Remove punctuation" on the same column) meant reopening the tool from the column menu each time, and closing it needed a trip to the × in the panel's top corner. Chao asked for the tool to stay open on the same column and for a Close button beside Cancel and Apply. Chao also once saw the second Clean text edit start from the original column, but could not reproduce it in the Tauri app; the backend chains both edits correctly through the API (checked), so nothing was changed for that.
- What changed:
  - `dataEditorToolStore` gains `formKey`, `resetForm(column)` and `setColumns(columns)`. `WorkspaceView` keys the panel by tool and `formKey`, so `resetForm` remounts a fresh form on the same column; `open` also bumps the key.
  - Apply success: toast, then `resetForm(column)` instead of `close()`. Settings other than the column return to their starting values (Clean text returns to the operation chosen from the column menu, or Trim), so the applied edit is not previewed a second time.
  - `useWorkspaceDataTable` keeps the open tool's columns equal to the active Data Block's committed columns (never preview or placeholder data), using the same filter as opening (`toolColumns`: every column for Copy column, text-readable columns otherwise).
  - Footer: Cancel (discards unapplied settings, keeps the tool open; disabled until something changes), Close (closes the tool), Apply. The header × stays.
  - Help: `tutorials/ui.md` Data Editor paragraph.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - After Apply in any Data Editor tool, the tool is still open on the same Data Block and column, with other settings reset, and the Data Block is not marked as having unfinished settings.
  - A second Apply on the same column builds on the first (Remove digits, then Remove punctuation, leaves neither).
  - A column added by the first Apply can be chosen in the tool, and its name is treated as taken.
  - Cancel is disabled on an unchanged form; after a change it restores the starting settings and keeps the tool open.
  - Close closes the tool.
- Implementation: `features/workspace/data-view/dataEditorToolStore.ts`, `components/DataEditorToolPanel.tsx`, `hooks/useWorkspaceDataTable.ts`, `components/layout/WorkspaceView.tsx`.
- Tests: `DataEditorToolPanel.test.tsx` ("stays open after Apply on the same column; Cancel starts over and Close closes", updated duplicate test); `useWorkspaceDataTable.test.tsx` ("keeps an open tool's columns in step with the Data Block after Apply"); manual Playwright WebKit run of the two-step Clean text flow.
- v0.8 notes: keep the tool open after Apply and refresh its column list from committed data, not the preview. Reset the form after Apply so a non-idempotent edit (Find & replace that adds text) is not previewed twice.

#### V07-148 Data Editor Clean text keeps the applied operation after Apply (#230)

- Commits: 848d449f
- Area: Data Editor
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Since #217 the tool restarts its form after Apply from its starting settings. For Clean text the starting operation is the one chosen from the column menu, so after applying lowercase from a tool opened as Title Case, the Cleaning list jumped back to Title Case.
- What changed: `resetForm(column, operation?)` sets the store's `initialOperation`; the panel passes the applied operation for Clean text only. Cancel returns to that operation too. Other settings and other tools still reset (#217).
- Expected behaviour (testable): open Clean text as Title Case, choose lowercase, Apply: the list still reads lowercase and the tool is not marked unfinished.
- Implementation: `features/workspace/data-view/dataEditorToolStore.ts`, `components/DataEditorToolPanel.tsx`.
- Tests: `DataEditorToolPanel.test.tsx` ("keeps the Clean text operation just applied rather than the starting one"); manual WebKit run.
- v0.8 notes: after Apply, keep a choice whose re-preview is harmless (idempotent operations); reset fields that would re-apply a non-idempotent edit.

### Data Builder

#### V07-012 Data Block Edits never change rows (#129)

- Commits: bb8fb01b
- Area: Data Builder (preprocessing), Data Blocks backend, Annotation, Docs
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: In-place edits replace a Data Block's plan, while annotations, analysis results and earlier descendants refer to its rows by position. A filter, sort, select or aggregation edit silently misaligned them. It also makes metadata colouring (#128) safe.
- What changed:
  - Each Data Builder tool has one fixed result destination, shown as a static line instead of the old "Apply result as" select (options were "New Data Block" / "Selected Data Block"): "Result: New Data Block" for Filter, Sample, Join, Stack (and analysis Add to Workspace, Workspace SQL Query); "Result: Updates the selected Data Block" for Find (Replace, Extract) and Create. Filter always shows the "New data block name" input and "Create Data Block" / "Creating Data Block…"; Create always shows "Updating Data Block…".
  - The "Expression" tool tab (CodeMirror typed Polars expression editor) is removed, with its hint, help anchors, tutorial entry and the `@codemirror/view` and `@uiw/react-codemirror` dependencies. Data Builder tabs are now Filter, Sample, Join, Stack, Find, Create.
  - Create's count/mean/sum column operations (the operation popover on column tokens and `.op()` chips) are removed; Create only builds new per-row columns.
  - Backend enforces: `FilterNodeEditRequest` is removed from `NodeEditRequest` (no `filter` edit kind); an `expression` edit accepts only `context: "with_columns"`; annotation Run All publication raises "Annotation Run All output does not keep the Data Block's rows" when the published row count differs. Kept in place: cast, rename_column, delete_column, replace, expression with_columns, set_cell. Documented exception: `annotation_classes` (codebook Data Block rows are the class list).
  - Older workspaces are not checked or migrated.
  - ADR 0031 `docs/adr/0031-data-block-edits-preserve-rows.md` supersedes the create-or-update clause of ADR 0007; `CONTEXT.md`, `docs/domain/workspaces-and-data-blocks.md`, `docs/reference/backend-api.md`, and tutorials `preprocessing.md`, `ui.md`, information `preprocessing.md` updated (screenshot `create_expression.png` deleted).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - No "Apply result as" select appears in any Data Builder tool; Filter shows "Result: New Data Block"; Find and Create show "Result: Updates the selected Data Block".
  - Applying a Filter always creates a new Data Block and leaves the source unchanged.
  - There is no "Expression" tab.
  - `POST /nodes/{id}/edits` with kind `filter`, or `expression` with context `filter`/`select`/`sort`/`group_by_agg`, returns a validation error (422).
  - Annotation Run All output with a different row count fails to publish.
- Implementation: backend `models/node_resources.py`, `services/node_operations.py`, `services/analysis_artifacts.py`; frontend `preprocessing/DataPreprocessingFeature.tsx`, `components/PreprocessingApplyBar.tsx` (prop `mode`), `preprocessingApplyMode.ts` (`UPDATE_DATA_BLOCK_MODE`), `filter/FilterSubTab.tsx`, `replace/ReplaceSubTab.tsx`, `aggregate/AggregateSubTab.tsx`, `aggregate/hooks/aggregateExpressionModel.ts`, `useAggregateSubTab.ts`, `common/hooks/useWorkspaceTransformMutations.ts`, `utils/autoNodeNames.ts`, `guidance/registry.ts`, `tutorials/bundledRegistry.ts`; deleted `expression/*` and `aggregate/components/OperationPopover.tsx`.
- Tests: new `backend/tests/unit/test_row_preserving_node_edits.py`; `test_canonical_node_api.py` (filter edit case removed); frontend `DataPreprocessingFeature.test.tsx`, `aggregateExpressionModel.test.ts`, `useWorkspaceNodeMutations.test.tsx`, `guidance/__tests__/registry.test.tsx` updated; expression and autoNodeNames tests removed.
- v0.8 notes: This is a core invariant: in-place edits never change row count or order, because results reference rows by position. In DuckDB, preserve a stable row identity (explicit row id column) and enforce the rule server-side.

#### V07-030 Data Preprocessing renamed Data Builder; Data Editor header fits narrow panels (#144)

- Commits: 980f492d (part)
- Area: Data Builder, Data Editor, Docs
- Type: wording / behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Name should pair with Data Loader (brings data in) and Data Editor (changes a block in place). Rejected: Corpus Builder, Collection Builder (protected terms), Transformer (clashes with AI models), Wrangler, Curator. On a small laptop screen the Data Editor Rename button overlapped Add column.
- What changed: Sidebar tab label "Data Builder" (view id stays `filter`), page heading "Data Builder", info icon label "About Data Builder" with tooltip "Learn how the Data Builder makes new Data Blocks for analysis.", subtitle "Make new Data Blocks from existing ones.", inputs panel title "Data Builder Inputs", Sample Data panel tool label "Data Builder". Help and tutorials updated. Data Editor header row wraps (`flex-wrap`), the name area has `min-w-48 basis-48`, so tools move to a second line.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Sidebar shows "Data Builder"; no user-visible "Preprocessing" label remains in the tab, heading, or hints.
  - At narrow widths the Data Editor tool buttons wrap under the block name and never overlap Rename.
- Implementation: `viewRegistry.ts`, `DataPreprocessingFeature.tsx`, `SampleDataPanel.tsx`, `WorkspaceDataHeader.tsx`, `tutorials/bundledRegistry.ts`, docs in `frontend/public`.
- Tests: `SidebarViewVisibilityMenu.test.tsx`, `viewRegistry.test.tsx`, `DataPreprocessingFeature.test.tsx` updated.
- v0.8 notes: Keep the three names Data Loader / Data Editor / Data Builder; internal ids (`preprocessing`, `filter`) were intentionally not renamed.

#### V07-034 Data Builder: Segment tool (#148)

- Commits: 0230a1e0 (part); renamed/subtitled in aac47e1d
- Area: Data Builder
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Segmentation existed only inside Topic Modelling; users wanted one row per sentence, paragraph, line, or speaker turn.
- What changed: New tab "Segment" (card subtitle from #159: "One row per sentence, paragraph, line, or pattern-led segment."). Uses the text column chosen in the inputs panel. "Split "<col>" into" radios: "Sentences" (hint "Ends at . ! ? or … followed by a space."), "Paragraphs" ("Separated by a blank line."), "Lines" ("Every line break starts a new segment."), "A pattern" ("A regular expression marking where each segment starts."). Pattern mode: "Each segment starts with" (placeholder "e.g. ^[\w\s]+:"; help "A regular expression; ^ is the start of a line. Text before the first match becomes segment 1."), "The matched text" radios "Goes into its own column" (name default "speaker") / "Is dropped, like a delimiter". Name default "<block>_<unit>s". Button "Create Data Block". Summary "N segment rows.". Preview title "Preview segments".
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Each output row keeps the source row's other columns and adds a "segment" column (1, 2, 3 ... within each source row), placed where the text column was (segment, [lead column], text).
  - Sentence split keeps the sentence's own punctuation and trailing quotes/brackets; "Dr. Smith" also splits (known difference from Topic Modelling's splitter).
  - Segments are trimmed and empty segments dropped; "\r\n" normalised to "\n".
  - Pattern "^[\w\s]+:" on a transcript puts "SPEAKER" (trailing ":" and spaces trimmed) into the "speaker" column; text before the first match becomes segment 1 with an empty lead.
  - Refused if the block already has a "segment" column or the lead column name is in use.
- Implementation: `SegmentDerivation` in `domain/workspace/provenance.py`, `SegmentNodeCreateRequest` in `node_resources.py`, `_segment` in `node_operations.py` (`_SENTENCE_END`, `_PARAGRAPH_BREAK`, marker split, `explode`, `int_range(...).over(row)`); provenance text "<unit> segments of <input>". Frontend `segment/SegmentSubTab.tsx`, `segment/segmentModel.ts`, shared `builder/BuilderToolCard.tsx` (inputs, form, name + Create, server preview, summary line), `createDerivedNode`/`previewDerivation` in `useWorkspaceTransformMutations.ts`. Previews of Segment/Aggregate/Deduplicate return `total_rows` (`_COUNTED_PREVIEWS` in `services/nodes.py`).
- Tests: `backend/tests/integration/test_data_builder_tools.py::test_segment_by_unit_and_by_speaker_pattern`; `test_data_editor_saved_plans.py::test_data_builder_plan_can_be_read_back`; `builder/__tests__/builderModels.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: Sentence rule was chosen because Topic Modelling's splitter lives in the polars-text plugin and cannot be saved in a v0.7 plan; v0.8 may use a proper sentence splitter, but results would then differ from v0.7. `(?m)` multiline regex is Rust-regex syntax.

#### V07-035 Data Builder: Group (formerly Split by group) (#149)

- Commits: 0230a1e0 (part); renamed in aac47e1d, icon rotated in 0473f26a, blank-text grouping in 161ccb2e
- Area: Data Builder
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Analyses that compare Data Blocks (for example per party or decade) needed several Filters.
- What changed: Tab (after #159) "Group", card title "Group into Data Blocks", subtitle "One Data Block per value, date period, or number range of a column.". "Split by" searchable column picker. Text/categorical: every value with its row count, most frequent first. Dates: "One Data Block per" "Year" / "Year and month" / "Day". Numbers: "Ranges starting at [0] of size [10]" or "Split the full range into [5] equal ranges". List header "Groups (n of m ticked)" with "Tick all" / "Untick all"; every group starts ticked; each row shows "N rows". "Name prefix" defaults to the block name; blocks are named "<prefix> · <group label>" (control chars to spaces, "/" and "\" to "-", ".." collapsed, max 120 chars, "(blank)" if empty). Null group label "(empty)". Number labels "0 to under 10", last equal range "X to Y" (inclusive). Button "Create N Data Block(s)"; at most 50 per run, otherwise "This column has more than 50 groups. Narrow the data first (for example with Filter), or use wider date periods or ranges." Preview shows the first ticked group ("Preview: <name>"). Month of year and Day of week were left out because Filter operators cannot express them.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Each created block is an ordinary Filter-derived block (lineage and provenance intact) with `eq` for values, `gte`/`lt` UTC ISO bounds for date periods, `gte`/`lt` (or `lte` for the last equal range) for numbers, `is_null` for the empty group.
  - With 51+ distinct values, no groups are listed and Create is disabled.
  - After #166, blank or whitespace-only text is counted in the "(empty)" group.
- Implementation: frontend-only orchestration: `split-group/splitGroups.ts` (`groupCountSql` via workspace SQL, `rangeSql`, `toGroups`, `groupBlockName`, `MAX_GROUPS = 50`), `split-group/SplitByGroupSubTab.tsx`, `builder/useBuilderSql.ts` (read-only workspace SQL, page size 500). Workspace SQL in v0.7 is Polars `SQLContext` (`services/workspace_sql.py`), using `STRFTIME`, `FLOOR`, `CAST AS VARCHAR`.
- Tests: `test_data_builder_tools.py::test_split_by_group_counts_and_filters_each_group`; `builderModels.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: SQL strings are written for Polars SQL; DuckDB syntax is similar (`strftime`, `floor`) but check argument order of `strftime` (DuckDB: `strftime(ts, fmt)` matches). Keep the 50-block cap, the " · " naming, and "every group starts ticked". The name "Group" was chosen over "Split" (clashes with Split column and Segment) and "Stratify" (reads as stratified sampling / social stratification).

#### V07-036 Data Builder: Aggregate (formerly Group & summarise) (#150)

- Commits: 0230a1e0 (part); renamed in aac47e1d
- Area: Data Builder
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: One document per speaker (texts joined) or counts and date ranges per group.
- What changed: Tab "Aggregate", subtitle "One row per group, such as one document per speaker, with each column summarised.". "Group by" chips with a searchable "Choose a column" / "Add a column" picker and per-chip remove ("Stop grouping by <col>"). "Summarise the other columns" list (with "Find a column" filter when more than 8 columns), each with a summary select. Options by type: text "Leave out", "Join text", "Count distinct", "Distinct values", "First", "Last"; numbers "Leave out", "Sum", "Mean", "Minimum", "Maximum", "Count distinct", "First", "Last"; dates "Leave out", "Earliest & latest", "Earliest", "Latest", "Count distinct", "First", "Last"; boolean/other "Leave out", "Count distinct", "First", "Last". Cautious defaults: the inputs panel's text column "Join text", date columns "Earliest & latest", everything else "Leave out". "Put between joined texts": "A blank line" (default), "A new line", "A space", "A semicolon". Summary "N groups, each with a "rows" count." Preview "One row per group, in the order each group first appears."
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Output has the group-by columns, a "rows" count (named "row_count" if "rows" is a group column), then summaries named "<col>" for Join text and "<col>_<summary>" otherwise (e.g. "likes_sum", "created_earliest" and "created_latest" for Earliest & latest, "<col>_distinct_values", "<col>_count_distinct").
  - Groups keep first-appearance order.
  - Sum/Mean on a non-numeric column is refused; min/max on nested columns refused.
  - Distinct values drops nulls and keeps first-seen order.
  - Default name "<block>_by_<col1>_<col2>".
- Implementation: `GroupSummaryDerivation`, `ColumnSummary` in `provenance.py`; `_group_summary` in `node_operations.py` (`group_by(..., maintain_order=True).agg(...)`, `str.join`, `n_unique`, `unique(maintain_order=True)`); frontend `summarise/GroupSummarySubTab.tsx`, `summarise/groupSummaryModel.ts`.
- Tests: `test_data_builder_tools.py::test_group_summary_uses_per_column_summaries`; saved-plan round trip; `builderModels.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: DuckDB `GROUP BY` does not preserve first-appearance order by default; order by `min(rowid)` to match. `string_agg` order must follow source row order. Keep the cautious defaults so ids are not summed or joined by surprise.

#### V07-037 Data Builder: Deduplicate (formerly Remove duplicates) (#151, #158)

- Commits: 0230a1e0 (part), 55f163a2 (#158 rework), aac47e1d (rename), ee4f6794 (list layout)
- Area: Data Builder
- Type: feature, then behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Deduplication existed only as an option inside Stack. #158: the inputs panel's "Text column" looked like the comparison basis but was only used for near-duplicate text.
- What changed (final state in this range): Tab "Deduplicate", subtitle "Keep the first of each duplicate, and save the duplicate groups separately.". The inputs panel's column is labelled "Deduplicating column:" and is always compared. "Additional columns to include (n of m)" checklist, empty by default, with "Select all" / "Select none" (and "Find a column to include" filter when more than 8 columns); help "Rows are duplicates when they match on "<col>"[ and every ticked column]. Select all to compare whole rows." The list is bordered, capped at 12rem height, and uses `grid-cols-[repeat(auto-fill,minmax(12rem,1fr))]` (about four per row on a laptop). "Match near-duplicate text in "<col>"" (enabled only when the deduplicating column holds text; else "Available when the deduplicating column holds text."), help "Texts match when they are the same after ignoring case, spacing, and punctuation.", optional "Also ignore web links and @mentions (for "RT @user:" re-posts)". Name label "Name the new data blocks". Button "Create 2 Data Blocks" creates "<name>_deduplicated" and "<name>_duplicates". Summary "Removes N duplicate rows; M rows kept." or "No duplicates found.". The original "Every column / Chosen columns" choice was removed by #158.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - "_deduplicated" keeps the first row of each duplicate group in original order.
  - "_duplicates" holds every row that has a duplicate (including the kept one), first columns "duplicate_group" (dense rank by first occurrence) and "kept" (true for the kept row), sorted by group then original order.
  - qldelection2020 deduplicated on "text" removes 166 of 2,380 rows; with Select all (whole rows) no duplicates are found.
  - Near-duplicate key: lowercase, (optionally) remove URLs/www/@mentions and a leading "rt", remove `[\p{P}\p{S}]`, collapse whitespace, trim.
  - Refused if the block already has "duplicate_group" or "kept" columns.
- Implementation: `DeduplicateDerivation` (columns, near_text_column, ignore_links_mentions, output "kept" | "duplicates") in `provenance.py`; `_deduplicate`, `_near_text_key` in `node_operations.py` (`unique(subset, keep="first", maintain_order=True)`; window `len().over(keys)`, `min(row).over(keys)`, `rank("dense")`); frontend `dedupe/DedupeSubTab.tsx`, `dedupe/dedupeModel.ts` (`buildDedupeBodies`: columns = [basis, ...ticked additional]), `DataPreprocessingFeature.tsx` column label; source row count via workspace SQL `COUNT(*)`.
- Tests: `test_data_builder_tools.py::test_deduplicate_keeps_first_and_exposes_duplicate_groups`; saved-plan round trip; `builderModels.test.ts` updated in 55f163a2.
- v0.8 notes: Issue design said names like "<block> (deduplicated)"; implemented as "<name>_deduplicated" / "<name>_duplicates". MinHash similarity matching (as in ATAP document-similarity) was deferred. DuckDB: use `row_number() OVER (PARTITION BY keys ORDER BY rowid)` for keep-first; ensure stable original order.

#### V07-038 Filter on text columns no longer coerces number-like values (#149 side fix)

- Commits: 0230a1e0 (part)

- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_filter_text_values.py::test_numeric_looking_values_match_by_column_type`.
- v0.8 notes: Type-driven coercion; DuckDB will implicitly cast in comparisons, so bind parameters with the column's type to avoid the same bug.

#### V07-044 Tab accepts the suggested value in every generated-name input (#156, race fixed in #164)

- Commits: ee9baf2c, 7c6879b2
- Area: Data Builder, Data Editor, Data Loader (data folder setup)
- Type: behaviour change / bug fix

Missing: new Data Builder tool names (including the Group prefix), the Count column name, and the suggested data folder. Later the #164 test caught a race: typing fast after Tab let a late caret restore land mid-word (" v2" became "2 v").
- What changed: `acceptPlaceholderOnTab` now also serves textareas and is wired to `BuilderToolCard` name input, Data Editor fields with `acceptPlaceholder` (Count name, and via #164 the new-column names), and `DataRootSetupForm` (only when there is a real suggested path, not the example path). The rAF caret restore runs only if the field still has focus and its value is still exactly the accepted suggestion.

#### V07-052 Filter: "is empty" covers missing, NaN, and blank text (#166)

- Commits: 161ccb2e
- Area: Data Builder (Filter, Group)
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: User feedback: "is null" did not match cells shown as "(empty string)" because they hold "" rather than a missing value. Null and "" differ to data scientists but not to HASS researchers.
- What changed: The Filter operator `is_null` is labelled "is empty" in every type's operator list; with Negate it means "is not empty". It matches null, NaN (float columns), and empty or whitespace-only text (String, Categorical, Enum); `is_not_null` is its exact negation. Stored operator names are unchanged, so saved filters keep working. Auto-generated filter names use "is_empty". Group's value list folds blank text into the "(empty)" group (SQL `CASE WHEN TRIM(...) = '' THEN NULL`).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Filter text "is empty" matches null, "", and "   " rows.
  - Filter score "is empty" on a Float column matches null and NaN.
  - Negate gives exactly the complement.
- Implementation: `_empty_value_expression` and `_condition_expression` in `node_operations.py`; `utils/typeUtils.ts` (`getOperatorsForField`), `utils/autoNodeNames.ts`, `split-group/splitGroups.ts`; `tutorials/preprocessing.md`.
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_filter_text_values.py::test_is_empty_treats_blank_text_and_nan_as_missing`.
- v0.8 notes: Saved plans store `is_null` / `is_not_null` with the new wider meaning; v0.8 must map them to the same semantics. DuckDB: `x IS NULL OR trim(x) = ''` for text, `isnan(x)` for floats.

#### V07-066 Join type selector: explanation on one row; Cross removed; Semi/Anti renamed; Left first (#179, #186)

- Commits: c22f7ce0, 8be8a0ae
- Area: Data Builder (Join)
- Type: behaviour change / wording
- Problem / reason: The selector showed bare values (inner, left, right, full, semi, anti, cross) on a half-width row with explanations only in the help page (#179). Chao then decided (#186) that Cross ignores the join columns, makes little sense for text data and can create huge blocks, so it was removed; Semi and Anti were renamed to plain words; Left, the default, is listed first.
- What changed:
  - One row: "Join type" label, help icon, selector (width `w-44`), and a description paragraph (`id="join-type-description"`, linked by `aria-describedby`) showing the chosen type's explanation.
  - Options in order, label then description:
    - "Left": "Keeps every row of the left data block and adds matching values from the right; rows without a match get empty cells."
    - "Inner": "Keeps only rows that match in both data blocks."
    - "Right": "Keeps every row of the right data block and adds matching values from the left; rows without a match get empty cells."
    - "Full": "Keeps every row of both data blocks, matched where possible; missing values are left empty."
    - "Keep matches" (value `semi`): "Keeps the left rows that have a match in the right, without adding any right columns."
    - "Keep non-matches" (value `anti`): "Keeps the left rows that have no match in the right."
  - Default join type is `left`.
  - Cross is gone from `JoinType` and the options; the cross-join warning banner and all "no columns needed" branches were removed, so join columns are always required. The backend still supports cross, so saved Projects with a cross join still load.
  - Auto output names: `<left>_matching_<right>` for semi, `<left>_not_matching_<right>` for anti, `<left>_<type>_join_<right>` otherwise (whitespace replaced with `_`).
  - Tutorial: "The first data block you add is the left block, and the second is the right block."; table rewritten without "null", with examples for the two Keep options.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - The selector's first option is "Left" and it is selected by default.
  - No "Cross" option exists.
  - Choosing "Keep matches" shows its explanation beside the selector and names the output e.g. `speeches_matching_speakers`.
  - With no join columns chosen the status says "Choose the columns that should match between the two data blocks."
  - Ready message is always "Ready to join <A> and <B> on <col> = <col>."
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/preprocessing/types.ts` (`JoinType`, `JOIN_TYPE_OPTIONS` with `label` and `description`), `join/JoinSubTab.tsx`, `join/hooks/useJoinSubTab.ts` (removed `needsColumns`, auto-name logic), `public/tutorials/preprocessing.md`.
- Tests: none added (existing join tests adjusted implicitly; `directPreviewAdapters.test.tsx` uses `left`).
- v0.8 notes: Keep the plain-language labels and descriptions and the left-first order. Keep reading cross joins from saved v0.7 projects if migrating plans. DuckDB supports SEMI and ANTI joins natively (`SEMI JOIN`, `ANTI JOIN`).

#### V07-123 Data Builder: one feedback pattern, toasts (#205 item 18)

- Commits: 1d5264bd
- Area: Data Builder (all tools)
- Type: behaviour change / wording
- Problem / reason: the shared modal was titled "Alert" and was used both for errors and for Sample's success message (which included a UUID); Filter, Join and Stack gave no success message; errors read "Error applying filter/join/stack: Unknown error".
- What changed:
  - The shared "Alert" `AlertDialog` (title "Alert", button "OK") is removed. `onAlert(message, cause?)`: without a cause it shows an error toast with the message; with a cause it calls `toastError(cause, 'Try again.', { title: message })` so the backend reason and Details appear.
  - Create failures in every tool: toast title "Couldn't create the Data Block." with the reason (and Details).
  - Validation toasts: Filter "Please select a data block first" to "Choose a Data Block first."; "Please fill in all filter conditions" to "Fill in every condition first."; Join "Please select two different data blocks and matching columns to join." to "Choose two different Data Blocks and a column in each to join on."; Stack "Select at least two compatible data blocks to stack." to "Choose at least two Data Blocks with matching columns to stack."; "Pick at least two data blocks to stack." to "Choose at least two Data Blocks to stack.".
  - Success toasts: Filter, Join, Stack now show "Created {name}." (name from the create response via `createdBlockName`, falling back to the requested name or "the new Data Block"); Sample/Slice shows "Created {name}." (no ID; old "{Slice|Shuffle|Random sample} added to the Project: name (uuid)." in the alert dialog).
  - Not changed (still at the branch tip 4e081f40): Deduplicate "Added {base}_deduplicated and {base}_duplicates to the Project.", Segment and Aggregate "Added {name} to the Project.", Group "Added N Data Block(s) to the Project." So two success phrasings remain; the agreed decision was the toast "Created speeches_2020.".
- Expected behaviour (testable): no "Alert" dialog appears anywhere in the Data Builder; a successful Filter shows the toast "Created speeches_filtered." (or whatever the name is); a failed Join shows "Couldn't create the Data Block." with the backend reason and a Details link.
- Implementation: `preprocessing/DataPreprocessingFeature.tsx` (`handleAlert`), `builder/BuilderToolCard.tsx`, `builder/createdBlockName.ts` (new), `concat/hooks/useConcatSubTab.ts`, `filter/hooks/useFilterSubTabSections.tsx`, `join/hooks/useJoinSubTab.ts`, `slice/hooks/useSliceSubTab.ts`, prop types in other sub-tabs.
- Tests: `builder/__tests__/createdBlockName.test.ts`; `AnnotationInferenceSettings.test.tsx` touched.
- v0.8 notes: use one pattern: success toast "Created {name}." and failure toast "Couldn't create the Data Block." with reason and Details. Consider unifying the remaining "Added … to the Project." messages.

#### V07-124 Join: clashing columns take the second Data Block's name (#205 item 19)

- Commits: 7ab3eb64
- Area: Data Builder Join (backend)
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: a column name present in both Data Blocks got the suffix "_right" in the joined result.
- What changed: Polars join (all types including cross) now uses `suffix=f"_{right.name}"`, for example `speaker_members` when the right block is `members` (a name with spaces gives e.g. `party_Topics again`). Join type labels (Left / Inner / Right / Full with one-line explanations) and Filter wording stay as they are, as agreed. Help preprocessing.md: "When both blocks have a column with the same name, the right block's copy is named after that block, for example `speaker_members` when the right block is `members`."
- Expected behaviour (testable): joining `speeches` (left) with `members` (right), both having `speaker`, gives columns `speaker` and `speaker_members`; no column ends in `_right`.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/node_operations.py` (join in `build_derived_lazyframe`).
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_topic_coverage_columns.py::test_join_suffixes_clashing_columns_with_the_second_data_block_name`.
- v0.8 notes: Polars-specific `suffix=` argument; in DuckDB SQL, clashing names must be aliased explicitly (`right.col AS "col_<right name>"`), and the result must still be unique if the suffixed name already exists (v0.7 does not handle that case explicitly; unclear what Polars does then).

#### V07-078 Title bar: Wordflow and LDaCA link to their websites; external links open on desktop (#171)

- Commits: 3fa0e130
- Area: Title bar, Desktop (Tauri)
- Type: behaviour change / bug fix
- Problem / reason: #171 asked for a link to the desktop downloads. An earlier commit (3adcf16f, outside this range) added a download icon to the web build only. Chao asked instead for the name and logo to be links. While testing, the desktop app was found never to have granted `opener:allow-open-url`, so the opener plugin's click handler for `target="_blank"` links failed silently and every external link (tokenizer docs, LDaCA collection links) did nothing on desktop.
- What changed:
  - The download icon is removed.
  - The "Wordflow" name links to `https://sih.tools/wordflow` (title "Wordflow website (opens in a new tab)"); the LDaCA logo links to `https://www.ldaca.edu.au/` (title "LDaCA website (opens in a new tab)"). Both open in a new tab (web) or the system browser (desktop), and both are excluded from the Tauri drag region (`data-tauri-drag-region="false"`). The logo link stays hidden below 700 px width as before.
  - Tauri capability `opener:allow-open-url` granted for `https://*` and `mailto:*` only.
  - `information/general.md` explains the links.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Clicking "Wordflow" in the title bar opens sih.tools/wordflow; clicking the logo opens ldaca.edu.au.
  - Dragging the title bar still moves the desktop window; clicking the links does not start a drag.
  - On desktop, any `target="_blank"` https link opens in the system browser; http (non-TLS) links are not allowed by the capability.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/components/layout/DesktopNavigationHeader.tsx` (`WORDFLOW_HOME_URL`, `LDACA_HOME_URL`; removed `showDesktopAppLink`), `frontend/src-tauri/capabilities/default.json`, `frontend/scripts/desktop-contract.test.mjs`.
- Tests: `DesktopNavigationHeader.test.tsx` ("links the Wordflow name and LDaCA logo to their websites"), desktop contract test; `cargo check` validates the capability.
- v0.8 notes: If v0.8 keeps Tauri, keep the opener URL allow-list, otherwise external links silently fail on desktop.

#### V07-084 Project Graph: Change Project menu in the title bar (#192)

- Commits: 290e5d99
- Area: Project Graph
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Switching projects meant going to the Data Loader to close one and open another (Chao's request). Chao revised the running-task rule: keep the Data Loader's rule (block) rather than offering to cancel.
- What changed:
  - A "Change Project" outline button (ArrowLeftRight icon, chevron) at the right end of the Project Graph title bar opens a dropdown listing the other projects ("No other projects" if none).
  - Choosing one opens a confirm dialog: title `Switch to “<name>”?`, description `“<current>” is saved automatically and will be closed.`, buttons "Cancel" and "Switch" (shows "Switching…" while working). The backend closes the current project (saved continuously) and opens the chosen one via `setCurrentWorkspace`. Success toast `Switched to “<name>”.`; error toast with the message or `Could not open “<name>”.`
  - While any Analysis in the current project is queued or running, every project item is disabled and a note shows: `A task is still running in “<current>”. Wait for it to finish, or stop it in its tab, before switching projects.` (plural form: `<n> tasks are still running in “<current>”. Wait for them to finish, or stop them in their tabs, before switching projects.`). The check is repeated on confirm (warning toast if a task started meanwhile).

#### V07-087 Project Graph: Pan / Select drag toggle replaces Overview; scroll pans (#194)

- Commits: 60d7e5b1
- Area: Project Graph
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: The graph was hard to pan: wheel or two-finger scroll zoomed (React Flow default); there was no box selection; the Overview (minimap) button was not useful.
- What changed:
  - Two-finger scroll or mouse wheel pans (`panOnScroll`); pinch or Ctrl/Cmd + scroll zooms.
  - The Overview button and minimap are removed and replaced by a drag-mode toggle: "Drag to pan" (default, Hand icon; accessible label "Drag to pan. Switch to drag to select") and "Drag to select" (SquareDashedMousePointer icon, shown as active; accessible label "Drag to select. Switch to drag to pan").
  - Pan mode: drag pans (`panOnDrag=true`), Shift + drag draws a box. Select mode: drag draws a box (`selectionOnDrag`), right or middle button pans (`panOnDrag=[1,2]`), and the pane's context menu is suppressed.
  - `SelectionMode.Partial`: blocks the box touches are highlighted while dragging and join the app selection when released, the same as clicking them (added to existing selection, not replacing). React Flow's group-drag rectangle (`.react-flow__nodesselection`) is hidden so it does not block clicks.
  - Drag mode is component state (not persisted).
  - ui.md updated.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Wheel/two-finger scroll moves the view; Cmd + scroll zooms.
  - In Select mode, dragging a box over two unselected blocks adds both to the selection without moving the view.
  - Right-drag in Select mode pans without showing the browser menu.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/workspace/graph-view/components/WorkspaceGraphFeature.tsx` (`GraphDragMode`, ReactFlow props), `graph-view/hooks/useWorkspaceGraph.ts` (`boxSelectionRef`, `handleNodesChange`, `handleSelectionStart`, `handleSelectionEnd` calling `replaceSelectedNodes` and `markInteracted`).
- Tests: `WorkspaceGraphFeature.test.tsx` ("pans by default and switches dragging to a selection box", "keeps the clear-selection action wired from the relocated rail"), `useWorkspaceGraph.test.tsx` ("adds the blocks a selection box touches to the current selection").
- v0.8 notes: React Flow specific props. Checked and confirmed in Safari.

Each button's name shows in a tooltip to the right after a 500 ms hover (`skipDelayDuration` 300 ms, `disableHoverableContent` so it ignores the pointer). Disabled buttons still show their tooltip (a wrapping span catches the pointer; the button itself gets `pointer-events-none` when disabled). The count tooltip reads `<n> of <total> data blocks selected`. Accessible labels are unchanged.

#### V07-092 Tasks panel: tasks named by tool and tab, a tab's finished steps combined, go to the tab (#199)

- Commits: 2dab9e41
- Area: Tasks panel, analysis tabs
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: The Tasks panel listed one row per stored analysis, named after the request kind ("Token Frequency", "Concordance Run All", "Concordance Document Data Block Creation"). With several tabs, rows could not be told apart, one tab could fill the list, and there was no way to get from a task to its tab. Each tab action stores its own analysis (Preview, Run All, Add to Project).
- What changed:
  - Names: `<short tool> - <tab name>`, short tools "Freq", "Conc", "Trends", "Topic", "Quote", "Annot" (e.g. "Freq - 1", "Topic - JP vs AUS"); names follow tab renames. Unknown tab: just the tool name (tool inferred from the request kind prefix), or "Analysis".
  - All successful analysis tasks of one tab share one row (key `tab:<tabId>`) whose details list each step, newest first, with its label ("Preview" for concordance/quotation/annotation previews, "Run" for other main runs, "Run All" for `*_run_all`, "Add to Project" for `*_data_block_creation`), its data block names, and its finish time.
  - Failed, cancelled, queued and running tasks keep their own row labelled `<name> · <step>` (e.g. "Conc - 2 · Run All").
  - Order: failed and cancelled first, then queued and running, then successful, newest first within each group.
  - A go-to arrow button after the expand arrow opens the task's tab (tooltip and label `Open <name>`), or the Data Loader for file imports ("Open the Data Loader"). Clicking elsewhere on the row toggles details. File imports stay one row each, labelled `<Task type>: <name>`.
  - Tab names: new tabs are plain numbers, one more than the largest numbered tab ("1", "2", "3"); the first auto-created tab is "1". Tabs saved as "Analysis N" display as "N" everywhere (tab strip, title picker, stop-word list sources e.g. "Frequency · 1").
  - Long names use `MiddleFadeLabel`: when too long, the first two characters, a half-faded third, then as much of the end as fits (fading in); full name in the title and for screen readers (e.g. "this is my analysis JP vs AUS" shows as "To[p]…alysis JP vs AUS").
  - ui.md updated.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - After a Frequency run in tab 1 the row reads "Freq - 1".
  - A Concordance tab with Preview, Run All and Add to Project successful shows one "Conc - 1" row listing three steps.
  - A failed Run All shows its own row "Conc - 1 · Run All" at the top.
  - The arrow opens the tab with tooltip "Open Freq - 1" without toggling details.
  - Renaming the tab renames the row.
  - A legacy tab "Analysis 3" shows as "3", and the next new tab is "4".
- Implementation: new `frontend/src/components/layout/sidebar/taskRows.ts` (`buildTaskRows`, `taskStepLabel`, `kindFromRequest`), `sidebar/MiddleFadeLabel.tsx`, rewritten `sidebar/SidebarTasksSection.tsx`, `layout/Sidebar.tsx` (`openTaskTarget` using `rememberActiveTab` and `setCurrentView`), `features/views/common/analysisNavigation.ts` (`shortLabel`, `displayTabTitle`, `nextTabTitle`), `tabs/tabStateOps.ts`, `tabs/useWorkspaceTabs.ts`, `tabs/AnalysisTabsHost.tsx`, `common/utils/stopWordListSources.ts`, `workspace/task-stream/taskProjection.ts` (`request_kind`, `node_ids` via `requestNodeIds`).
- Tests: `SidebarTasksSection.test.tsx` (naming, combined row, go-to button, terminal failure), `analysisNavigation.test.ts` ("names tabs with plain numbers and shows legacy \"Analysis N\" as N"), updated `AnalysisTabsHost.test.tsx`, `stopWordListSources.test.ts`, `DesktopNavigationHeader*.test.tsx`.
- v0.8 notes: A follow-up 065b192b (outside this range) folds a Run All's per-block child analyses into the parent row. The step labels depend on v0.7 request kind names; map v0.8 task kinds equivalently. Checked in WebKit.

#### V07-096 Tasks panel: a Run All folds its per-Data-Block analyses into one row (#199)

- Commits: 065b192b
- Area: sidebar Tasks panel
- Type: bug fix / behaviour change
- Problem / reason: a Run All (renamed "Run" later the same day, see the #205 wording entry) stores one parent analysis plus one supporting analysis per Data Block. A two-block Run All therefore showed as three task rows (the parent, and each block failing or finishing separately).
- What changed:
  - Supporting analyses whose `parent_analysis_id` points to a task present in the list are no longer shown as rows of their own.
  - The parent's row, when expanded, shows a list (accessible name "`<row label>` data blocks", for example "Conc - 2 · Run All data blocks") with one item per child: a status icon, the Data Block name(s) in bold (falls back to the step label), and for a failed child ": `<failure message>`".
  - When a row has block results, the older single "step label: blocks" line is not shown.
  - Help: ui.md explains that a Run with two Data Blocks shows as one task and each block's result is listed when opened.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A Run All over two Data Blocks (parent failed, one child failed with "cannot cast", one child successful) produces exactly one row, labelled "Conc - 2 · Run All" (after #205: "Conc - 2 · Run"; after #211 the tab part becomes e.g. "C-2 · Run").
  - Expanding it lists "Q1 JP: cannot cast" and "Q1 UK".
  - A child whose parent is not in the task list still shows as its own row.
- Implementation:
  - `frontend/src/features/workspace/task-stream/taskProjection.ts`: `analysisToTask` sets `parent_analysis_id` only when `resource.execution_scope === 'supporting'`.
  - `frontend/src/components/layout/sidebar/taskRows.ts`: `buildTaskRows` builds `childrenByParent`, skips children, adds `blockResults: TaskRowStep[]` to `TaskRow`.
  - `frontend/src/components/layout/sidebar/SidebarTasksSection.tsx`: renders the `<ul>` of block results with `statusMeta(state)` icons.
- Tests: `frontend/src/components/layout/sidebar/__tests__/SidebarTasksSection.test.tsx`, new describe "SidebarTasksSection Run All grouping (issue 199)".
- v0.8 notes: the v0.7 model stores one parent plus one supporting analysis per input; if v0.8 keeps per-input sub-jobs, the task list must still present one user action as one task with per-input results and per-input failure reasons.

#### V07-099 Batch delete: untick Data Blocks in the confirmation to keep them (#204)

- Commits: bab80a63
- Area: Project Graph, "Delete (n)" control
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: the confirmation listed the selected blocks as plain names; to spare a block selected by mistake the user had to cancel, deselect it in the graph or sidebar, and start again.
- What changed:
  - Each listed block has a checkbox, all ticked when the dialog opens (the kept set is reset on every open). The list has accessible name "Data blocks to delete"; each checkbox is labelled by the block name. Unticked names are shown in the muted colour.

The following data blocks will be removed:"). The #205 consistency pass later capitalised "Data Block(s)" in these strings (see that entry; check the current file).

#### V07-135 macOS title bar: tinted glass strip so its items stay readable (#214)

- Commits: ed9a9ad8
- Area: Desktop
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: On macOS the title bar is Clear Liquid Glass (ADR 0030) and every item on it (Wordflow name, About and Cite icons, "by", back and forward, Settings) was forced to white. Clear glass shows whatever is behind the window, so over a white or light desktop the items nearly vanished, more so when the window was inactive. Reported by Chao on 0.7.8-dev with screenshots over light and dark backdrops, active and inactive.
- What changed:
  - The title bar strip (`app-glass-titlebar`, the header only) takes `color-mix(in srgb, var(--vscode-titleBar-activeBackground) 88%, transparent)` over the glass. The white foreground overrides were removed, so its items use the theme's normal title bar colours, as in the browser build.
  - The gutters around the cards stay untinted glass; the window frame and sidebar wrapper are unchanged.
  - The tint is CSS rather than the plugin's `tintColor`, so it follows a theme change without reinitialising the native effect. ADR 0030, `docs/architecture/frontend/desktop.md` and the desktop runbook were amended.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - With native glass active, the Wordflow name, About, Cite, "by", back, forward and Settings are readable over a white desktop and a dark one, in light and dark themes, with the window active and inactive.
  - The gutters around the cards still show the glass.
  - Browser, Windows, Linux and a failed glass start keep the solid title bar.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/index.css` (`html[data-native-glass="active"] .app-glass-titlebar`); `components/layout/DesktopNavigationHeader.tsx` (class renamed from `app-glass-titlebar-foreground`); ADR 0030 and the desktop docs.
- Tests: `DesktopWindowFrame.test.tsx`, `scripts/desktop-contract.test.mjs` (class contract); manual Playwright WebKit shots with the glass marker set over white, dark and colourful backdrops in both themes (the native glass itself cannot run in Playwright).
- v0.8 notes: never put a fixed white (or any fixed) foreground on see-through window material; give the strip a theme tint and use theme tokens. Check it on the real macOS app over a white desktop.

#### V07-138 Tab strips scroll to show the active tab (#218)

- Commits: 30a6b7f2
- Area: Project Graph, Tasks panel and layout (shared `EditorTabs`)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: With several Data Blocks open in the Data Editor, the tab strip showed its left end, so the active tab (usually the newest, on the right) was hidden. `EditorTabs` never scrolled to the active tab, and a remount (switching views) starts at scrollLeft 0.
- What changed:
  - `EditorTabs` has an effect keyed on the active tab id, its computed slot (`positions`, `widths`), the strip width and drag state. It scrolls just enough to show the active tab, including the strip's 8 px side padding. A tab wider than the strip shows its start. It never runs on a manual scroll, and not during a drag.
  - Shared by the Data Editor, analysis tab strips and the Data Builder, so all of them follow.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - With more tabs than fit, the strip shows the active tab when it first renders, after switching to another view and back, and when another tab becomes active or a new tab is added.
  - Scrolling the strip by hand is not undone until the active tab or the layout changes.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/components/tabs/EditorTabs.tsx` (`STRIP_SIDE_PADDING`, active-tab effect).
- Tests: `EditorTabs.test.tsx` ("scrolls to show the active tab when it mounts and when it changes"); manual Playwright WebKit run with eight Data Blocks open, switching views and back.
- v0.8 notes: any horizontally scrolling tab strip must bring the active tab into view on mount and on activation.

#### V07-145 Analysis tools: visible vertical resize handles (#227)

- Commits: df70232d
- Area: Layout (analysis tools)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: The handle between an analysis tool's settings and results was three faint 2 px dots that faded on hover, and each result chart's grip was a tiny corner mark; Chao could not see either.
- What changed:
  - `ResizeHandle` gains `variant="bar"`: a 32 × 4 px rounded bar in `foreground/25`, darker on hover or focus, always visible. `AnalysisSplitLayout` uses it.
  - `ResultFrame` grip: the same bar, centred on the bottom edge; the frame keeps a 10 px strip (`pb-2.5`) free and gives its chart 10 px less, so the grip covers nothing.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A short grey bar marks the settings/results split and the bottom of each result chart, in light and dark themes.
  - Dragging and double-clicking behave as before.
- Implementation: `components/layout/ResizeHandle.tsx`, `features/views/common/components/AnalysisSplitLayout.tsx`, `ResultFrame.tsx`.
- Tests: existing ResultFrame and AnalysisSplitLayout suites; manual WebKit shots.
- v0.8 notes: resize handles must be visible without hovering.

#### V07-146 Dropdowns: no scroll arrows (#228)

- Commits: 1a7769a6
- Area: Layout (shared `components/ui/select.tsx`)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: A small down arrow under dropdown lists that already fit suggested more options. It was the Radix Select scroll button, shown because WebKit's fractional heights (and the open animation's scale) made a fitting list look 1 px too tall.
- What changed: the scroll-up and scroll-down buttons are removed; lists scroll with the wheel, trackpad or scrollbar.

#### V07-152 Tasks panel: tool prefix on every task; imports open their folder and state the outcome (#235)

- Commits: 4ae62af8
- Area: Project Graph, Tasks panel and layout
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Task names came from the tab's display name, so a tab named after a word (a Concordance opened from a word in Frequency is named "yeah") showed no tool prefix, unlike "C-2". Import tasks had no prefix, and a finished import's arrow opened the Data Loader in general although its record keeps where the files went.
- What changed:
  - `taskTabName(name, kind)` (`analysisNavigation.ts`): default names stay "C-2"; any other name reads "C - yeah". Running rows read "C - yeah · Run". Tabs keep their own names.
  - Import tasks: "L - Sample data import", "L - LDaCA collection import: <name>"; L for Loader. E is reserved for Export, which has no background tasks yet.
  - `importToTask` keeps a finished import's outcome (`destination_path`, `file_count`, `bytes_written`) and sets the message "Imported 12 files (48 MB) to sample_data/ADO/reddit".
  - A finished import's target is `{ kind: 'data-loader', folder }`; the Sidebar sets `fileRevealStore` and switches to the Data Loader; `FileTree` opens the folder and its parents, selects it, scrolls to it and clears the request (state updates in an animation frame, per the React Compiler rule). Running or failed imports open the Data Loader as before.
  - Sort order unchanged (failed and cancelled, then queued and running, then successful; newest first). Help: `tutorials/ui.md` Tasks panel.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A Concordance tab named "yeah" shows as "C - yeah" in the Tasks panel; "C-2" stays "C-2"; renaming the tab updates the task name.
  - Imports read "L - Sample data import"; a finished one shows its outcome sentence and its arrow opens and selects its folder in the Data Loader.
- Implementation: `components/layout/sidebar/taskRows.ts`, `components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `features/workspace/task-stream/taskProjection.ts`, `features/views/data-loader/components/FileTree.tsx`, new `stores/fileRevealStore.ts`, `features/views/common/analysisNavigation.ts`.
- Tests: `analysisNavigation.test.ts` (taskTabName), `taskProjection.test.ts` (outcome and message), `taskRows.test.ts` (folder target), `FileTree.test.tsx` (reveal), `SidebarTasksSection.test.tsx` (names); manual WebKit run on the isolated instance.
- v0.8 notes: keep one naming convention for every task (tool prefix plus name; L for Loader, E for Export), and make every go-to lead to the task's outcome.

### Export

#### V07-055 Export: CSV readable in Excel, Excel (.xlsx) export, NDJSON and Arrow removed (#169)

- Commits: 678f6ed9, b5736fdf (backend/uv.lock); follow-ups 9b47a1bb (CI allow-list) and 3d3a74e7 (time zones in Excel) are outside this range
- Area: Export (Data Blocks, Token Frequency, Topic Modelling), Build/CI
- Type: bug fix / feature / behaviour change
- Problem / reason: User feedback: a Concordance CSV showed "that â€™ s focused on" in Excel because no CSV export wrote a UTF-8 BOM, so Excel read Windows-1252. Users asked for files usable in other software. NDJSON and Arrow IPC were judged unnecessary as export formats.
- What changed: All CSV exports start with a UTF-8 BOM (Data Block `sink_csv(include_bom=True)`; frontend `csvBlob` for Token Frequency and Topic Modelling topics CSV). New Data Block export "Excel (.xlsx)": one worksheet "Data", header row, no autofit, written in memory with `DataFrame.write_excel` (xlsxwriter) through the same byte budget. Refused with HTTP 400 if rows + header exceed 1,048,576 ("This Data Block has N rows, more than an Excel worksheet holds (1,048,575). Export it as CSV or Parquet instead.") or any text cell exceeds 32,767 characters ("Excel cells hold at most 32,767 characters, and some text in 'col' is longer. Export it as CSV or Parquet to keep the full text."). List and struct columns are written as JSON text in CSV and Excel (CSV export used to fail on them). Format list now "CSV (.csv)", "Excel (.xlsx)", "JSON (.json)", "Parquet (.parquet)"; NDJSON and Arrow IPC removed from the backend enum, OpenAPI, frontend list, Tauri `save_data_block_export` allow-list (`csv | xlsx | json | parquet`), and docs. Loading .ndjson and .arrow files is unchanged. `xlsxwriter>=3.2.0,<4` added and locked in both `uv.lock` and `backend/uv.lock` (b5736fdf, because CI syncs the backend lock with `--locked`).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Every CSV download begins with bytes EF BB BF (frontend CSV text begins with "﻿").
  - An xlsx export round-trips text and numbers.
  - Requesting format "ndjson" or "ipc" is rejected.
  - A list column exports as JSON text like `["a","b"]`.
- Implementation: `DataBlockExportFormat` in `models/node_resources.py`; `_write_lazyframe`, `_flatten_nested_columns` (struct json_encode wrapper then strip), `_excel_workbook_bytes` in `services/data_block_exports.py`; `api/workspaces/nodes.py` response media types; `frontend/src/lib/download.ts::csvBlob`; `features/workspace/common/dataBlockExport.ts`; `tokenFrequencyExport.ts`; `TopicModelingBubbleChartSection.tsx`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/download.rs`; docs `information/export.md`, `tutorials/export.md`.
- Tests: `backend/tests/integration/test_data_block_exports.py` (`test_csv_export_starts_with_a_utf8_byte_order_mark`, `test_data_block_export_no_longer_offers_ndjson_or_arrow`, `test_excel_export_explains_when_a_block_is_too_large`), `backend/tests/unit/test_data_block_export_formats.py` (nested JSON, xlsx round trip, cell and row limits); `tokenFrequencyExport.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: xlsx export was removed by Alex in July (commits 2d93f43, 8c23244, 61faad6, 5531191f, no reason recorded; likely because Excel cannot be streamed like `sink_*`). It is back at users' request; the issue asks Alex whether collecting the whole block conflicts with memory limits and whether DuckDB's `excel` extension suits v0.8. Unlike CSV/Parquet it is not streamed (memory = collected frame + workbook).

#### V07-075 Export: Excel writes time-zone-aware date-times as UTC (#169 follow-up)

- Commits: 3d3a74e7
- Area: Export
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: xlsxwriter refuses zoned datetimes ("Excel doesn't support timezones in datetimes"), so exporting a block with a zoned datetime column to Excel failed. (#169 itself, the UTF-8 BOM, new Excel export and removal of NDJSON/Arrow IPC export, was done in 678f6ed9, outside this range.)
- What changed: For Excel only, zoned Datetime columns are converted to UTC and the zone dropped (UTC wall-clock). CSV, JSON and Parquet keep the zone. Export docs: "Excel has no time zones, so date-times are written in UTC in Excel files; CSV, JSON, and Parquet keep the time zone."
- Expected behaviour (testable): A column at 2020-10-17 02:00 Australia/Brisbane exports to Excel as 2020-10-16 16:00; a UTC value 15:00 stays 15:00.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/data_block_exports.py` `_excel_workbook_bytes` (`dt.convert_time_zone("UTC").dt.replace_time_zone(None)`).
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_data_block_export_formats.py::test_excel_writes_zoned_datetimes_as_utc`.
- v0.8 notes: Polars-specific transform; in DuckDB use `timezone('UTC', ts)` to a naive TIMESTAMP before writing xlsx. Keep the doc statement true. See the #169 comment to Alex about Excel export design (collected, not streamed; capped by Excel's 1,048,576 rows and 32,767 characters per cell).

### Data folder, storage and runtime

#### V07-015 Startup snapshot cleanup is best effort, never blocks the Data Root (#131)

- Commits: c7b15a76 (test typing follow-up in 1a9e6ad2)
- Area: Storage / Data Root (backend runtime), desktop (Windows)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: A Windows desktop user was stuck on the first-start Data Root screen ("Choose another Data Root") with `PermissionError: [WinError 5] Access is denied: '...\.response-snapshots\resources'`. The doubled backslashes are only Python's repr. Startup ran `shutil.rmtree` on abandoned response snapshots (`ResponseSnapshotService.reconcile()`); Windows refuses to delete files another process holds open (a second backend on the same Data Root, antivirus/Defender, the indexer). Any error aborted Runtime initialization and the UI reported "Data Root required".
- What changed: Startup cleanup of `.response-snapshots` and of abandoned query snapshots is best effort: locked paths are logged (warning) and left for the next start instead of aborting Data Root initialization. This reverses the earlier "propagate cleanup failure" rule. Trade-off: a locked orphan may use some disk until a later start removes it.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - If a file under `.response-snapshots` cannot be deleted at startup, the Runtime still opens and a warning "Left N abandoned response snapshot path(s) that could not be removed, first: <path>" is logged.
  - If the query snapshot root cannot be fully removed, a warning "Abandoned query snapshots could not be fully removed from <path>" is logged and startup continues.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/response_snapshots.py` (`shutil.rmtree(root, onexc=skip_locked)`, collects OSError paths; `onexc` needs Python 3.12+); `services/analysis_results.py` (try/except OSError around `_remove_query_root`).
- Tests: new `backend/tests/unit/test_startup_snapshot_cleanup.py`; `test_storage_admission.py` now asserts skip-and-log. 1a9e6ad2 uses `monkeypatch` for the run helper to satisfy `ty`.
- v0.8 notes: Treat all snapshot/cache cleanup as best effort on Windows. Follow-up suggested on the issue (not done): the Tauri shell has no single-instance guard (`tauri-plugin-single-instance`), so launching twice starts a second backend on the same Data Root.

#### V07-016 Open the default Data Root on first start (#130)

- Commits: 1ebdb6b4
- Area: Data Root / Settings (backend runtime), Docs
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: First start asked users to choose between the recommended location and another folder, a decision they had no basis for.
- What changed: Without `DATA_ROOT` or saved configuration, a single-user backend opens the platform's suggested Data Root (e.g. `%LOCALAPPDATA%\au.edu.ldaca.wordflow\data` on Windows) without asking, and saves it to `settings.json` once it opens (if saving fails, it is logged and repeated next start). Users can still move it under Settings. The chooser appears only if the default cannot open, showing the real error. Multi-user and environment-managed deployments are unchanged. UI tutorial text: "On first launch without `DATA_ROOT` or saved configuration, Wordflow uses the recommended location for your operating system and remembers it. There is nothing to choose." and "If that location cannot be used (for example, it is not writable), a setup screen shows the error and lets you choose another folder."
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Fresh single-user start with no config: no Data Root prompt; `settings.json` afterwards contains the suggested root; the runtime source becomes "config".
  - If the default root cannot be opened, the setup screen shows the actual error and lets the user pick another folder.
  - Multi-user mode still requires configuration as before.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/runtime.py` (`_RuntimeOwnerRequest.persist`, `_initialize_owned(..., persist=True)` writes via `self._config_store.write`, uses `self._config_store.paths.suggested_data_root`).
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_data_root_bootstrap.py` (default opens, default fails).
- Docs: `docs/architecture/backend/runtime.md`, `docs/architecture/frontend/desktop.md`, `docs/reference/backend-settings.md`, `frontend/public/tutorials/ui.md`.
- v0.8 notes: Keep zero-question first start for the desktop/single-user app.

#### V07-149 Windows: staging paths kept under 260 characters (#232)

- Commits: 365c548a
- Area: Data folder, storage and runtime
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: On a fresh Windows install, adding a Parquet file (or a folder of them) failed with FileNotFoundError (workspace_corrupt, HTTP 500). A Project snapshot staged under the default Data Root reached 261 characters (`…\workspaces\.staging\.snapshot-<Project id>-<32 hex>\data\.<Data Block id>.r2-<32 hex>.plbin.<random>.tmp`); Windows refuses paths over 259 characters unless long paths are enabled, which is off by default. The naming predates v0.7, so 0.7.7 had the same bug.
- What changed: generated names are short and keep their leading dot and suffix (cleanup and quota code rely on both): staging folder `.snapshot-<12 hex>`; `atomic_output_path` temporaries `.t-*.tmp` (no longer repeating the target's name); input snapshot staging `.s-*.tmp`; uploads `.u-*.upload`; plan validation copies `.v-*.validate` (not `.plbin`, which the data-folder scan deletes); sample downloads `.d-*.download`.
- Expected behaviour (testable): the reported path becomes 127 characters; temporary names are at most 16 characters whatever the target; adding files works on Windows under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\au.edu.ldaca.wordflow\data` with a long user name.
- Implementation: `infrastructure/storage/{durable_fs,input_snapshots,safe_paths,workspace_store}.py`, `services/{sample_data,workspace_coordination}.py`.
- Tests: `tests/unit/test_windows_path_budget.py`; full backend suite.
- v0.8 notes: budget every generated path for Windows' 260-character limit; never build temporary names from the target's full name.

#### V07-150 Desktop: a slow first start is allowed; plain startup messages (#231)

- Commits: af5f19ee
- Area: Data folder, storage and runtime (desktop shell)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: A fresh install on a 2-CPU Windows VM showed "Backend did not complete startup before the deadline"; the second start worked. The desktop bundle has no precompiled bytecode for its libraries (7,870 `.py`, bytecode only for Python itself) and runs with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, so every start compiles every imported module, and a fresh install also waits for antivirus scans. Measured on an M-series Mac: cold import 7.7 s as shipped, 0.7 s with bytecode.
- What changed: `STARTUP_TIMEOUT` 30 s → 180 s (a crashed backend still fails at once through the exit check). Timeout detail: "Wordflow took too long to start. This can happen the first time on a slow computer."; dialog title "Wordflow couldn't start", ending "Close this message and open Wordflow again. If it keeps happening, please send feedback."
- Expected behaviour (testable): a first start that takes up to three minutes succeeds; a backend crash still shows the dialog at once.
- Implementation: `frontend/src-tauri/src/backend_process.rs`, `supervisor.rs`; runbook `docs/runbooks/desktop-runtime.md`.
- Tests: `cargo test` (27 passed); manual check on the Windows VM with the next build.
- v0.8 notes: precompile bytecode (or use a native server) so starts are fast; keep a generous first-start allowance.

#### V07-151 Windows: short names for sources an Analysis stages (#233)

- Commits: db26554b
- Area: Data folder, storage and runtime
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: After #232, Topic Modelling still failed on Windows with FileNotFoundError [WinError 3]: an Analysis staged each source as `<64-hex SHA-256 of its path>.parquet` under `analyses\<id>\.execution\<tmp>\sources`, a 265-character path under the default data folder. The Python backend is not long-path aware on Windows unless `LongPathsEnabled` is set machine-wide. The hash names each staged source uniquely within one Analysis; it is not a content fingerprint.
- What changed: 16 hex characters instead of 64 (`input_snapshots.py`). The reported path becomes 217 characters (222 with a 20-character user name). Shipped by rebuilding only the v0.7.8 Windows installer (Chao's decision): MSI, signature and the Windows entry of `latest.json` replaced, and a one-line notice added to the release notes; macOS, PyPI and the tag unchanged.
- Expected behaviour (testable): Topic Modelling and other Analyses run on Windows under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\au.edu.ldaca.wordflow\data`; staged source names are at most 16 hex characters plus the extension.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/infrastructure/storage/input_snapshots.py`.
- Tests: `test_worker_input_snapshots.py` (real staged names), `test_windows_path_budget.py` (path budget).
- v0.8 notes: Rust's standard library uses extended-length paths on Windows automatically, so v0.8 should not hit this, as long as file access goes through `std::fs`. For any remaining Python in v0.8, budget paths or use `\\?\` paths.

#### V07-126 Help: one plain "How Preview, Run and Clear work" section; fix help that contradicts the UI (#205 items 23 and 24)

- Commits: 35fc5dbb
- Area: help pages (tutorials, information), help registry
- Type: docs
- Problem / reason: item 23: the Preview/Run/Clear lifecycle was explained in developer terms ("immutable snapshot", "projection", "Analysis forest", "root", "Supporting Analysis", "mutable Data Block") across several pages, and draft notes were visible (a stray "Token Frequency" line, "[anything else you can change?]", "link to LDACA site?"). Item 24: help contradicted the UI.
- What changed:
  - New ui.md section `#help-ui-preview-run-clear` "How Preview, Run and Clear work" (registry key `ui.preview-run-clear`), linked from every analysis tool page. Content: Preview works out a first few pages; Run works through all data (needed for sorting every column, whole-result charts, and before Add to Project); Clear removes this tab's Preview and Run results but not Data Blocks already added nor changes written to a Data Block (use Undo in the Data Editor). What to expect: results keep the data they were made from (edits later do not change them; Preview/Run again); buttons turn on only when a relevant setting changes (and off again if changed back); settings are locked while a task runs, use Stop; after a failure or a stop, settings can be edited but Preview and Run stay off until Clear; results stay with the tab across tool switches and Project reopen.

Views", "2. Data Blocks", "3. Tasks"; anchors unchanged `help-ui-tool-choice`, `help-ui-data-selection`, `help-ui-task-centre`; registry labels updated).
- Expected behaviour (testable): every analysis tool help page links to "How Preview, Run and Clear work"; searching help for "snapshot", "projection", "Analysis forest" or "Supporting Analysis" finds nothing.
- Implementation: `frontend/public/tutorials/*.md`, `frontend/public/information/*.md`, `frontend/src/tutorials/bundledRegistry.ts`.
- Tests: `frontend/src/tutorials/__tests__/documentationRegistry.test.ts` (label update).
- v0.8 notes: the lifecycle rules described here (results are snapshots, buttons enable on relevant change only, Clear required after failure/stop, results persist per tab) are the v0.7 behaviour contract; if v0.8 changes any of them, update this section.

#### V07-132 Docs submodule bump to the published v0.7 docs channel (#119)

- Area: `ldaca-wordflow-docs` submodule

#### V07-061 Test stability and type-check fixes (no issue)

- Commits: 6e4a9b2a, 58bc9b2a
- Area: Build/CI
- Type: test-only
- Problem / reason: 6e4a9b2a: `getAnalysisResource` was unmocked, so the handoff query made a real request from jsdom; on a slow CI runner it could settle before the assertion that Run All stays active until the forest adopts it. 58bc9b2a: CI runs Ty over the tests; `dict` is invariant, so request fixtures typed `dict[str, object]` failed.
