#### V07-064 CI: xlsxwriter allowed again; macOS desktop build fix (no issue; follows #169)

- Commits: 9b47a1bb
- Area: Build/CI
- Type: build/CI
- Problem / reason: `scripts/verify_backend_distribution.py` listed `xlsxwriter` as a removed dependency (the old Excel export was dropped in 299d00b4), but #169 brought Excel export back, so every backend CI job failed. In `desktop-build.yml`, expanding an empty bash array under `set -u` fails on macOS bash 3.2 ("unbound variable"), so a macOS build from checked-out sources (without `--no-sources`) failed in "Prepare backend runtime".
- What changed: `xlsxwriter` removed from the banned list; the pnpm call uses `${args[@]+"${args[@]}"}`.
- Expected behaviour (testable): Backend CI passes with xlsxwriter declared; macOS desktop build with `NO_SOURCES` false passes the "Prepare backend runtime" step.
- Implementation: `.github/workflows/desktop-build.yml`, `scripts/verify_backend_distribution.py`.
- Tests: none (CI itself).
- v0.8 notes: If v0.8 keeps Excel export via xlsxwriter, keep it out of any removed-dependency guard. The bash 3.2 empty-array idiom applies to any macOS runner script.

#### V07-081 CI: six Windows-only backend test failures (#142)

- Commits: 5e55b49a
- Area: Build/CI (backend storage)
- Type: build/CI (with two small production fixes)
- Problem / reason: Six backend tests failed only on the Windows runner (POSIX mode bits, reading a held lock file, path separators).
- What changed:
  - Tests: mode bits (0o600) checked only where they exist; the lock file is read after release and the secret scan skips lock files (Windows byte-range locks are mandatory); plan source paths compared as paths.
  - Production: on Windows a link or reparse point at a workspace lock entry now raises `OSError(errno.ELOOP, "Workspace lock entry is a link or reparse point", path)` (wrapped in `WorkspaceLockStorageError`), matching POSIX `O_NOFOLLOW`; plan relocation compares `Path` objects instead of strings, because Polars stores `C:/...` forward-slash paths on Windows, so Windows no longer rewrites every plan on load or rebase.
  - Not changed: Windows lock files stay unreadable to other tools while a Project is open; private TOML files on Windows rely on the Data Root folder's ACL rather than mode bits.
- Expected behaviour (testable): CI run 36221984547 green on Windows, Linux, macOS; opening a Project on Windows does not rewrite unchanged plans.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/infrastructure/storage/workspace_lock.py` (`acquire_workspace_lock`), `workspace_store.py` (`_rebase_plan_copy`, `_read_workspace`), `input_snapshots.py` (`rebase_worker_input_snapshot_sources`).
- Tests: `test_analysis_service.py`, `test_data_root_bootstrap.py`, `test_private_toml.py`, `test_workspace_lock.py`, `test_workspace_store.py` made platform-aware.
- v0.8 notes: Polars plan path storage is Polars-specific; any stored file paths in DuckDB-era projects should also be compared as paths, not strings, on Windows.

#### V07-085 Version 0.7.8-dev for desktop test builds (no issue)

- Commits: 0ae5433e
- Area: Build/CI
- Type: build/CI
- Problem / reason: Needed a distinguishable version for desktop test builds. Windows MSI (WiX) requires a numeric version.
- What changed: Version set to `0.7.8-dev` in `backend/pyproject.toml`, `pyproject.toml`, `frontend/package.json`, `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, `tauri.conf.json`, locks and OpenAPI. The Windows build step writes a temporary Tauri config setting `bundle.windows.wix.version` to `<major.minor.patch>.0` when the version has a non-numeric prerelease, and passes it with `--config`. The health test compares against the installed `__version__` instead of a literal.
- Expected behaviour (testable): Windows MSI of 0.7.8-dev packages as 0.7.8.0; `/health/live` returns the package version.
- Implementation: `.github/workflows/desktop-build.yml` (PowerShell `$buildArgs`), `backend/tests/integration/test_app_factory_runtime.py`.
- Tests: `test_liveness_and_readiness_are_distinct_and_legacy_health_is_absent` updated.
- v0.8 notes: Keep the WiX numeric-version workaround for prerelease desktop builds.

## Planned for v0.8 (new features, not in v0.7)

Designed with the product owner and written up as issues; build them on v0.8 from the specs in the issues.

- **Code designer (#53).** AI-assisted codebook design in the Annotator: a description plus a seeded sample of distinct texts (default 30, at most 200; optional stratification by up to 2 columns, at most 6 groups of at least 20) produce a new Codebook table, a linked examples Data Block (`text`, `code`, `reason`, `correction`) and a summary; redesign rounds from the researcher's corrections; design rows left out of reliability; provenance and a copyable methods note; structured, validated AI output. The agreed spec is in the issue.
- **Annotator deduplication (#234).** On by default: each distinct text (trimmed) is coded once and its label joined back to every copy; review and reliability count distinct texts, with the basis stated. One open question (copies that already disagree) is noted in the issue.

## Points the recorders could not confirm

- e37bb048: the segmentation and HDBSCAN Auto-cap rules live in polars-text 0.6.1; the entry follows the issue text, not the Rust code.
- 12970c61: about 330 strings were renamed from Workspace to Project; the entry quotes a sample.
- 54bf549d / ad6148fd: LDaCA (Oni) provider internals such as paging and the concurrency limit come from commit messages and issue comments.
- 0230a1e0: Group's date bounds were not checked on time-zone-aware datetime columns other than UTC.
- 73005897 (#189): whether relaxed bubble positions can end up outside the graph canvas.
- 6101fb8f (#197): how %DIFF sorts rows set to N/A, and whether the keyness download also replaces the no-Reference value.
- 919cb08c (#181): sheet names for files ticked inside a folder rely on each file being created as its own request (inferred from docs).
- 2e72095d, 35fc5dbb, 4d85698e: help-page wording edits were skimmed, not read line by line; screenshots in 57c47fc5 were not inspected by the recorder.
- 7ab3eb64: what a Join does when the suffixed name (`col_<right name>`) already exists in the left Data Block.
